\tag {20}
$$

Naturally, there is no asymmetry in $\sigma(\tau)$ . The dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ is shown in Fig.2 for several values of Q.

![](images/1b7ffdcf3a86d09e9a2953e40a0196a8b8d4ea16c8d7eab29b8b93214871675a.jpg)

</details>

Figure 1. Dependence of $\Sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (top plot) and $\Sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (bottom plot) on $\vartheta$ for f=2 (solid curve), f=3 (dashed curve) and f=4 (dotted curve), $f=Q\cos\tau$ .   
![](images/11b6cb6bb6e89aace75756a2cc1fe8d9b6b31902b1ce08266c810ecedfead7f2.jpg)

<details>
<summary>line</summary>


</details>

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

# 前端小站

# 文章：什么时候该用 $< \mathrm{a}>$ ？

当你的意图是“导航到某个资源”时，用链接很合适；如果是触发动作（比如打开弹窗），通常更该用<button>。

小提示：你也可以用链接跳到页面内的某个位置，比如跳到联系信息。

utions, suggesting that their initial anionic states possess opposite parities. Since peak 5 has been identified as the transitions from the anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ ( $^{4}F_{5/2}^{\text{e}}$ ) of to the atomic ground state Th ( $^{3}F_{2}$ ) [30], peak 4 is assigned to the transition from the lowest anionic odd-parity state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}G_{5/2}^{\mathrm{o}})$ to the same neutral state Th $(^{3}F_{2})$ now. This transition was predicted by high-precision calculations [30] and had not been experimentally observed previously due to the resolution limitation. To assign these peaks from anionic excited states, we changed the trap time to analyze the intensity trends of the different peaks. The intensity of a peak originating from an excited state with a shorter lifetime exhibit faster intensity decay with increasing trap time. We collected a series of energy spectra with different trapped time (blue curve for 45ms, purple for 5ms) and the no-trap mode (red for no-trap). Peaks 1 and 6 correspond to be the transition from the new anionic state $^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{o}}$ to the Th ground state. Peak 3 was previously assigned as the transition from the long-lived anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ [30]. And peaks 2, 7 and 10 are now assigned to the transitions from the same new anionic state $^{4}F_{9/2}^{\mathrm{e}}$ to different neutral Th states. Note that the peak 8 near peak EA in Fig.2 is a transition from Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ to Th $(^{3}F_{3})$ , with a measured binding energy of 4878.8(61) cm $^{-1}$ . The energy gap between peak 8 and 9 is only 20 cm $^{-1}$ , highlighting the high resolution near the photodetachment threshold.

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

# 文章：什么时候该用 $< \mathrm{a}>$ ？

当你的意图是“导航到某个资源”时，用链接很合适；如果是触发动作（比如打开弹窗），通常更该用<button>。

小提示：你也可以用链接跳到页面内的某个位置，比如跳到联系信息。

# 作者联系信息

邮箱：zhangsan@example.com

电话：+86 138 0000 0000

地址（可选）：上海市某某路100号（地图）

utions, suggesting that their initial anionic states possess opposite parities. Since peak 5 has been identified as the transitions from the anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ ( $^{4}F_{5/2}^{\text{e}}$ ) of to the atomic ground state Th ( $^{3}F_{2}$ ) [30], peak 4 is assigned to the transition from the lowest anionic odd-parity state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}G_{5/2}^{\mathrm{o}})$ to the same neutral state Th $(^{3}F_{2})$ now. This transition was predicted by high-precision calculations [30] and had not been experimentally observed previously due to the resolution limitation. To assign these peaks from anionic excited states, we changed the trap time to analyze the intensity trends of the different peaks. The intensity of a peak originating from an excited state with a shorter lifetime exhibit faster intensity decay with increasing trap time. We collected a series of energy spectra with different trapped time (blue curve for 45ms, purple for 5ms) and the no-trap mode (red for no-trap). Peaks 1 and 6 correspond to be the transition from the new anionic state $^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{o}}$ to the Th ground state. Peak 3 was previously assigned as the transition from the long-lived anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ [30]. And peaks 2, 7 and 10 are now assigned to the transitions from the same new anionic state $^{4}F_{9/2}^{\mathrm{e}}$ to different neutral Th states. Note that the peak 8 near peak EA in Fig.2 is a transition from Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ to Th $(^{3}F_{3})$ , with a measured binding energy of 4878.8(61) cm $^{-1}$ . The energy gap between peak 8 and 9 is only 20 cm $^{-1}$ , highlighting the high resolution near the photodetachment threshold.

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

$$

Naturally, there is no asymmetry in $\sigma(\tau)$ . The dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ is shown in Fig.2 for several values of Q.

![](images/1b7ffdcf3a86d09e9a2953e40a0196a8b8d4ea16c8d7eab29b8b93214871675a.jpg)

Figure 1. Dependence of $\Sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (top plot) and $\Sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (bottom plot) on $\vartheta$ for f=2 (solid curve), f=3 (dashed curve) and f=4 (dotted curve), $f=Q\cos\tau$ .   
![](images/11b6cb6bb6e89aace75756a2cc1fe8d9b6b31902b1ce08266c810ecedfead7f2.jpg)

<details>
<summary>line</summary>


</details>

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

# 前端小站

# 文章：什么时候该用 $< \mathrm{a}>$ ？

当你的意图是“导航到某个资源”时，用链接很合适；如果是触发动作（比如打开弹窗），通常更该用<button>。

小提示：你也可以用链接跳到页面内的某个位置，比如跳到联系信息。

utions, suggesting that their initial anionic states possess opposite parities. Since peak 5 has been identified as the transitions from the anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ ( $^{4}F_{5/2}^{\text{e}}$ ) of to the atomic ground state Th ( $^{3}F_{2}$ ) [30], peak 4 is assigned to the transition from the lowest anionic odd-parity state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}G_{5/2}^{\mathrm{o}})$ to the same neutral state Th $(^{3}F_{2})$ now. This transition was predicted by high-precision calculations [30] and had not been experimentally observed previously due to the resolution limitation. To assign these peaks from anionic excited states, we changed the trap time to analyze the intensity trends of the different peaks. The intensity of a peak originating from an excited state with a shorter lifetime exhibit faster intensity decay with increasing trap time. We collected a series of energy spectra with different trapped time (blue curve for 45ms, purple for 5ms) and the no-trap mode (red for no-trap). Peaks 1 and 6 correspond to be the transition from the new anionic state $^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{o}}$ to the Th ground state. Peak 3 was previously assigned as the transition from the long-lived anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ [30]. And peaks 2, 7 and 10 are now assigned to the transitions from the same new anionic state $^{4}F_{9/2}^{\mathrm{e}}$ to different neutral Th states. Note that the peak 8 near peak EA in Fig.2 is a transition from Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ to Th $(^{3}F_{3})$ , with a measured binding energy of 4878.8(61) cm $^{-1}$ . The energy gap between peak 8 and 9 is only 20 cm $^{-1}$ , highlighting the high resolution near the photodetachment threshold.

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-

# 前端小站

阅读文章 联系作者 MDN: <a> 文档（新窗口）

# 文章：什么时候该用 $< \mathrm{a}>$ ？

当你的意图是“导航到某个资源”时，用链接很合适；如果是触发动作（比如打开弹窗），通常更该用<button>。

小提示：你也可以用链接跳到页面内的某个位置，比如跳到联系信息。

# 作者联系信息

作者：张三

邮箱：zhangsan@example.com

电话：+86 138 0000 0000

地址（可选）：上海市某某路100号（地图）

客服：support@example.com·电话：+86 139 0000 0000

utions, suggesting that their initial anionic states possess opposite parities. Since peak 5 has been identified as the transitions from the anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ ( $^{4}F_{5/2}^{\text{e}}$ ) of to the atomic ground state Th ( $^{3}F_{2}$ ) [30], peak 4 is assigned to the transition from the lowest anionic odd-parity state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}G_{5/2}^{\mathrm{o}})$ to the same neutral state Th $(^{3}F_{2})$ now. This transition was predicted by high-precision calculations [30] and had not been experimentally observed previously due to the resolution limitation. To assign these peaks from anionic excited states, we changed the trap time to analyze the intensity trends of the different peaks. The intensity of a peak originating from an excited state with a shorter lifetime exhibit faster intensity decay with increasing trap time. We collected a series of energy spectra with different trapped time (blue curve for 45ms, purple for 5ms) and the no-trap mode (red for no-trap). Peaks 1 and 6 correspond to be the transition from the new anionic state $^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{o}}$ to the Th ground state. Peak 3 was previously assigned as the transition from the long-lived anionic excited state Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ [30]. And peaks 2, 7 and 10 are now assigned to the transitions from the same new anionic state $^{4}F_{9/2}^{\mathrm{e}}$ to different neutral Th states. Note that the peak 8 near peak EA in Fig.2 is a transition from Th $^{-}$ $(^{4}F_{7/2}^{\mathrm{e}})$ to Th $(^{3}F_{3})$ , with a measured binding energy of 4878.8(61) cm $^{-1}$ . The energy gap between peak 8 and 9 is only 20 cm $^{-1}$ , highlighting the high resolution near the photodetachment threshold.

Naturally, there is no asymmetry in $\sigma(\tau)$ . The dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ is shown in Fig.2 for several values of Q.

Figure 1. Dependence of $\Sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (top plot) and $\Sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (bottom plot) on $\vartheta$ for f=2 (solid curve), f=3 (dashed curve) and f=4 (dotted curve), $f=Q\cos\tau$ .

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

Dependence of $\Sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (top plot) and $\Sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (bottom plot) on $\vartheta$ for f=2 (solid curve), f=3 (dashed curve) and f=4 (dotted curve), $f=Q\cos\tau$ .

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Naturally, there is no asymmetry in $\sigma(\tau)$ . The dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ is shown in Fig.2 for several values of Q.

Figure 1. Dependence of $\Sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{s}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (top plot) and $\Sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)=\sigma_{0}^{-1}d\sigma_{a}(\vartheta,\tau)/d\vartheta$ (bottom plot) on $\vartheta$ for f=2 (solid curve), f=3 (dashed curve) and f=4 (dotted curve), $f=Q\cos\tau$ .

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in

Fig. 3. The time-averaged cross section grows logarith-  
![](images/edca25ca30932f8e03b27a90585e5b936b1255ad1c8b8995ae1eeba2bf1f08bc.jpg)

Figure 2. Dependence of $\Sigma_{tot}(\tau)=\sigma(\tau)/\sigma_{0}$ on $\tau$ for Q=1 (solid curve), Q=2 (dashed curve) and Q=3 (dotted curve).

The total cross section increases with $Q$ and is well approximated at $Q \gg 1$ by the formula

$$
\sigma (\tau) \approx \pi \sigma_ {0} \ln (1 + 2 Q | \cos \tau |).
$$

The total cross section averaged over time may also be of interest,

$$
\overline {{\sigma}} (Q) = \int_ {0} ^ {2 \pi} \sigma (\tau) \frac {d \tau}{2 \pi} = \pi \sigma_ {0} \int_ {0} ^ {1} [ 1 - J _ {0} (Q y) ] \frac {d y}{y}. \tag {21}
$$

The dependence of $\overline{\Sigma}_{tot}(Q) = \overline{\sigma}(Q) / \sigma_0$ on $Q$ is shown in
